An abundance of proof in the Bible and Tradition shows the promise of a Redeemer who would be capable of satisfying God's justice for the sins of mankindone powerful enough to reopen the gates of heaven. 

Jesus is referred to as the Redeemer because He brought us back from the brink of hell and by His atonement satisfied for our sins. Christ is a mediator, because He reconciled man to God. He is the intercessor because, unceasingly, He pleads for us with His heavenly Father. 

As a preamble to the doctrines which prove the Incarnation and the Redemption of Christ, recall the passages in the Old Testament which refer repeatedly to the Redeemer. During a period of many thousands of years the people longed for the Messias or the Christ- the 

Anointed One. Jesus the Messias had a threefold office: as prophet (that is, teacher), priest and king. 

To whom was the promise of the Messias made? 

God promised a Redeemer to the patriarchs, Abraham,

Isaac, Jacob and Juda, and also to King David. First, however, He made the promise to Adam before he left Paradise. God made the promise to Abraham when he moved to Chanaan; to Isaac, when famine threatened to force him out of Chanaan; to Jacob, when he fled from Esau; to Juda, at the death of his father, Jacob; and to King David, when he wished to build a temple as a climax to his reign. These facts show that the promises were renewed at important epochs of history. 

OUR REDEMPTION 

Various opinions obtain about the timing of the Redemption. Of course, we can only speculate, because

"For them also" nothing definite in The Book tells why God chose the time He did. Some writers opine that the long life granted to the patriarchs enabled them to repeat to their descendants the promise of a Redeemer. The seventy prophets listed in Holy Scripture gave many details about the coming of a Redeemer and His life, His teaching, and the miracles He would perform; and finally, the climax of His sufferings and death on the Cross. It appears that God intended certain types to foreshadow the Redeemer. The types here listed show some resemblance to our Saviour.

Adam, who is the father of all men in the flesh, resembles Christ, the Head of all in the spirit. Abel, the just man, was put to death by reason of envy. Noe saved his people in the ark (the symbol of the Church) and formed a covenant with God. Melchisedech offered a sacrifice of bread and wine, foreshadowing the Holy Mass. Isaac carried the wood for the fire intended for his own sacrifice. Jacob left a good home for distant lands, where he found his bride (type of the Church). 

Joseph, persecuted by his brethren, became their rescue in famine; the Paschal Lamb symbolized the freeing of men from temporal death; the manna which God sent from heaven nourished the Israelites in their travels.

Moses was the lawgiver and leader of his people. Josue led his people into the promised land; Gedeon was chosen by God to save his people through his own weakness; Samson looms victorious in death. King David suffered many tribulations and proved himself meek and humble; Solomon, the wise ruler, had a glorious reign. 

Jonas was detained three days in the belly of a whale.

The word "Jesus," from the Hebrew, signifies Saviour or Redeemer. Isaias, known as the Prince of prophets, prophesied in detail the coming of the Redeemer. "Surely He hath borne our infirmities and carried our sorrows: and we have thought Him as it were a leper, and as one struck by God and afflicted. But He was wounded for our iniquities: He was bruised for our sins. The chastisement of our peace was upon Him: and by His bruises we are healed. All we like sheep have gone astray, every one hath turned aside into his own way: and the Lord hath laid on Him the iniquity of us all. He was offered because it was His own will, and He opened not His mouth. He shall be led as a sheep to the slaughter and shall be dumb as a lamb before His shearer, and He shall not open His mouth." (Isaias 53:4-7) 

THE ANOINTED ONE 

This prophecy foretells the Redemption of mankind to be made by the Messias. Moreover, the prophets pointed out the time of our Redeemer's coming and of His death: "Seventy weeks [of years] are shortened upon thy people and upon thy holy city, that transgression may be finished and sin may have an end and iniquity may be abolished and everlasting justice may be brought and vision and prophecy may be fulfilled and the Saint of

Saints may be anointed. Know thou therefore and take notice: that from the going forth of the word to build up Jerusalem. again, unto Christ the prince, there shall be seven weeks and sixty-two weeks: and the street shall be built again, and the walls in straitness of times. And after sixty-two weeks Christ shall be slain: and the people that shall deny Him shall not be His. And a people, with their leader that shall come, shall destroy the city and the sanctuary: and the end thereof shall be waste, and after the end of the war the appointed desolation. [This reference foretells the destruction of 

Jerusalem by General Titus, in the year 70.] 

And He shall confirm the covenant with many, in one week: and in the half of the week the victim and the sacrifice shall fail: and there shall be in the temple the abomination of desolation: and the desolation shall continue even to the consummation and to the end." (Daniel 9:24-27) "For thus saith the Lord of hosts: 'Yet one little while, and I will move the heaven and the earth and the sea and the dry land. And

I will move all nations: AND THE DE- SIRED OF ALL NATIONS SHALL COME: 

and I will fill this house with glory.... Great shall be the glory of this last house more than

of the first,' saith the Lord of hosts: 'and in this place I will give peace.' (Aggeus 2:7-8, 10)

The expression "this last house" refers to the second temple. This was intended to console the Jews, who grieved because, when their temple was rebuilt, it seemed to them less magnificent than the one that Solomon had erected.

Isaias the prophet foretold something of the public life of Jesus. Speaking in the Lord's name, he said: "The Spirit of the Lord is upon Me, because the Lord hath anointed Me. He hath sent Me to preach to the meek, to heal the contrite of heart, and to preach a release to the captives and deliverance to them that are shut up: to proclaim the acceptable year of the 

Lord and the day of vengeance of our God: to comfort all that mourn." (Isaias 61:1-2) 

The prophets foretold that Jesus would be sold for thirty pieces of silver; that He would be reviled and spit upon; that He would be given gall and vinegar to drink; that He would be put to death.

The psalmist who sang centuries before the coming of the Messias also envisioned Christ's Resurrection: "... Thou wilt not leave My soul in hell: nor wilt Thou give Thy Holy One to see corruption." (Psalm 15:10)

When certain unbelievers tried to distort this passage and asserted that David referred to his own death, St. 

Paul refuted them: "For David, when he had served in his generation, according to the will of God, slept; and was laid unto his fathers and saw corruption." 

(Acts of the Apostles 13:36) 

The Ascension of our Lord, as well as the descent of the Holy Ghost upon the Apostles, were likewise foretold. 

Isaias further predicted the extension of God's kingdom among the Gentiles: ... I come that I may gather them together with all nations and tongues: and they shall come and shall see My glory. And I will set a sign among them, and I will send of them that shall be saved, to the Gentiles into the sea, into Africa and Lydia, . . . into Italy and 

Greece, to the islands afar off, to them that have not heard of Me and have not seen My glory.... And I will take of them to be priests and Levites,' saith the Lord." (Isaias 66:18-19, 21)

Jesus Christ was born and lived among us to teach us how to live that we might reach our true home in heaven.

He suffered and died on the Cross to redeem us from our sins, and so prove His love for us. We give great importance to the crucifix in our devotions because it is

the instrument of our redemption. We keep it always before us as a constant reminder of the strength of God's love for men and His intense desire for the salvation of souls.

He redeemed us and He will judge us, every man according to the way he has lived. 

"For the Son of man shall come in the glory of His Father with His angels; and then will 

He render to every man according to his works." (St. Matthew 16:27)

THE DIVINE ATONEMENT 

God (who is rich in mercy) for His exceeding charity wherewith He loved us even when we were dead in sins, hath quickened us together in Christ (by whose grace you are saved)." (Ephesians 2:4-5)

Inquirers pose the question: "Why did God favor mankind with redemption, in contrast with His prompt punishment of the fallen angels?" While no one pretends to know the mind of God, some theologians come up with the theory that more was expected of the angels, who were created with intellects so brilliant that they realized "like a sudden flash of lightning" the frightfulness of rebellion against their Maker. The fallen angels, quite conscious of their guilt, sinned directly and individually; mankind, on the other hand, did not sin directly nor individually, but inherited original sin from the first

parents of the human race. Although the guilt of original sin is removed in the Sacrament of Baptism, the effects remain. These are weakness of the will and proneness to evil. It would seem that the very weakness of man appealed to the mercy of God, rather than the arrogance of the group of angels, who in their pride defied Him. 

ACCEPT THE CROSS 

The profound reasons back of many of the doctrines are not always understood; but in the light of the guidance of the Holy Spirit we come to realize something of the beauty of the Cross and the merit of suffering patiently. In fact, the Cross can be described as a luminous ladder by which we ascend to heaven, even though the steps be painful. Where the Cross of Christ has a part in our life, it teaches divine love and sweet resignation under trial. From these sufferings blossoms forth the peace which surpasses understanding. This was the great gift vouchsafed to the saints and martyrs.

St. Paul teaches that the carnal man, on the other hand, does not perceive the things that are of the spirit of God. The carnal man is totally absorbed with self and base appetites. He has no time for the welfare of his soul or the eternal values of the spiritual. It is necessary, then, to pray before study and to beg the gift of wisdom, which will enlarge our vision and deepen our grasp of the truths which Christ taught to mankind. 

Before proceeding, it may help us to consider the answer to an oft-repeated question, Could Almighty God: have reconciled man without the Incarnation and Passion of His divine Son? And the answer is: Yes, God could

have accomplished this by virtue of His benevolence. 

All things are possible to our heavenly Father, so that He could have restored the title to heaven, which we hold as children of God, and He could have imbued us with sanctifying grace without the Incarnation of Jesus. 

God could have pardoned the offense or could have required some other type of satisfaction.

CONDIGN SATISFACTION 

On the other hand, keep in mind that an offense is measured according to the dignity of the person offended. 

It is a greater offense to offer insult, for example, to the President of the United States than to one of your fellow workers. In fact, if you offended the rulers of some countries, especially those countries enslaved by despots, it would cost you your life. 

Now, when a rational creature offends Almighty 

God, the difference in dignity is infinite. No matter what efforts man might make to balance the scales of divine justice, whatever he could accomplish would still be finite. 

He could never effect an atonement commensurate with a serious offense against God. Because our satisfaction for sin has only a finite value, it was necessary for a divine Person to atone for us. God could not do this in His divine nature, for expiation involves a certain amount of abasement incompatible with the divine. Therefore, to provide adequate satisfaction it was necessary to have a divine nature united with a human nature in the one Person of Jesus Christ to make the atonement, the fruits of which are infinite.

Consider another possibility. Any one of the divine 

Persons could have become man; but since we are adopted children of God, it would seem more appropriate that the Son of God should make amends for His adopted brethren.

REDEEMED BY HIS PRECIOUS BLOOD 

Christ willed to make adequate reparation for mortal sin, which had deprived Adam and his posterity of sanctifying grace, of adoption as children of God, and of the hope for eternal happiness in heaven. The loss of sanctifying grace kills the supernatural life of the soul and enslaves one to the devil. The atonement of Jesus freed man from this slavery by paying a ransom in His Precious Blood. That is the force of the word "redeem.” ee ...you were not redeemed with corruptible things..., but with the Precious Blood of 

Christ, as of a lamb unspotted and undefiled.” (1 St. Peter 1:18, 19) "Who His own self, bore our sins in His Body upon the tree..., by whose stripes you were healed." (1 St. Peter 2:24)

THE LAMB OF GOD 

In the Sacrifice of the Cross, Jesus was the Priest and the Victim offered. The Cross was the altar. St. John therefore refers to our Saviour as "the Lamb of God... who taketh away the sin of the world" (St. John 1:29).

That is why the adorable Sacrifice of the Mass is divine, for Jesus continues to offer the sacrifice under the appearance of bread and wine. Reflect, also, that our divine 

Redeemer continues to offer to His eternal Father in heaven satisfaction for our sins. Jesus redeemed us by 

His death on the Cross. ... Christ... by His own Blood entered once into the Holies, having obtained eternal redemption.... How much more shall the 

Blood of Christ, who by the Holy Ghost offered Himself unspotted unto God, cleanse our conscience from dead works?" (Hebrews 9:11, 12, 14)

Nor could this be counted a temporary atonement. Our 

Saviour has made Himself our permanent mediator. Ce ... there is one God; and one mediator of 

God and men, the man Christ Jesus." (1 Timothy 2:5) 

When our Saviour commissioned His Apostles to go into the whole world and "preach the Gospel to every creature," He longed to reconcile all mankind to God. 

He put at our disposal the infinite merits of His sacrifice on the Cross. Jesus "is the propitiation for our sins; and not for ours only, but also for those of the whole world" (1 St. John 2:2). "R as in Adam all die, so also in Christ all shall be made alive." (1 Corinthians 15:22)

LIMBO After the death of Jesus on the Cross, His soul descended into limbo, a place of waiting: "In which also coming He preached to those spirits that were in prison." (1 St. Peter 3:19)

Those who lived before the Incarnation and death of 

Jesus could be saved if they believed in the Redeemer who was to come, and kept the natural law. Christ is the 

Redeemer of all mankind, whether unbelievers know 

Him or not: "Neither is there salvation in any other." 

(Acts of the Apostles 4:12) 

You may observe that there are many in pagan lands, and even in our own land, who know nothing of the commandments or of God. Can such be saved? These also can save their souls, because it is the will of God that all men be saved and come to the knowledge of truth. But God does not expect the impossible. If such as described above are ignorant of the commandments, they surely know something of the natural laws which are written on the human heart. If they know nothing of Jesus the Redeemer, they can know from the light of their own minds the existence of a Supreme Being who rewards the good and punishes the wicked. This all men fairly well disposed, may discover. If we make a slight effort to know and love God and to regret sin, His mercy enfolds us.

Here a word seems in order regarding the adoration which we pay to the Sacred Body and Blood of Christ. 

When we worship these, we worship the Person who is God. This worship is called "latria." The teaching of the Church extends this same type of adoration to the 

Word made flesh. Jesus merits our profound homage.

When the priest distributes Holy Communion, he recites a prayer: "May the Body of our Lord Jesus Christ preserve your soul to life everlasting."

COURAGE To stress these truths and to find the encouragement that all of us need to lead a good Christian life, we are assured of the loving personal interest of Jesus Christ in each individual. We can still hark back to the prince of prophets, Isaias: "Say to the fainthearted: "Take courage, and fear not. Behold, your God will bring the revenge of recompense. God Himself will come and will save you. Then shall the eyes of the blind be opened: and the ears of the deaf shall be unstopped. Then shall the lame man leap as a hart, and the tongue of the dumb shall be free.'" (Isaias 35:4-6) 

The royal psalmist also envisions the Passion and death of Jesus, His Sacred Heart aflame with love and encircled with thorns of anguish: "O God, My God, look upon Me: why hast 

Thou forsaken Me?... All they that saw Me

have laughed Me to scorn: they have spoken with the lips and wagged the head. 'He hoped in the Lord, let Him deliver Him: let 

Him save Him, seeing He delighteth in 

Him....' They have dug My hands and feet: they have numbered all My bones. And they have looked and stared upon Me. They parted My garments amongst them: and upon 

My vesture they cast lots. But Thou, O Lord, remove not Thy help... from Me.... I will declare Thy name to My brethren.... All the ends of the earth... shall be converted to the Lord." (Psalm 21:2, 8-9, 17-20, 23, 28)

Even though Jesus loves all of us with undying love, His merits and satisfactions are never forced on anyone.

We must respond to His love. The fruits of Christ's death on the Cross are real spiritual gifts that lift mankind to a supernatural status, assuming, of course, that we accept these gifts. No matter how often God knocks at the door of a heart, He cannot come in- in virtue of our free will unless we invite Him. The beginning and the end of our justification depend entirely on the merits of Christ's suffering and death. But we must appropriate to ourselves the merits of the Cross. To this end, God exhorts us: "Turn ye to Me,... and I will turn to you." (Zacharias 1:3)

"Convert us, O Lord, to Thee, and we shall be converted." (Lamentations 5:21)

Even though we are weak, the efficacy of the grace of God will not be lost upon us. We must make use of the means of grace lavished on us by God. To make use of the means of grace would include keeping God's commandments. Our Lord commissioned the Apostles not only to teach all nations and to baptize them but also to observe what He had commanded. The Apostles and their successors, the bishops of the Church, therefore, were commissioned to teach what Christ had taught them.

Chapter XV THE RESURRECTION 

OU are aware, from reading the Sacred Scripture, You that whenever our Lord mentioned His death, He added something about His Resurrection. He permitted 

His enemies to plot against Him and put Him to death. 

He confirmed the faith of believers by His triumph over death and sin. The Resurrection of our divine Saviour foreshadows the resurrection of the just. Adam, the father of the human race, brought death by his transgression. Christ, the second Adam, made eternal life available to all.

St. Paul teaches: "If there be no resurrection of the dead, then Christ is not risen again" (1 Corinthians 

15:13). In the sight of God the Father, the human nature of Christ in which He suffered is a constant oblation offered for our salvation. St. Paul's brilliant instruction on the Resurrection emphasizes the vital importance of this point.

The sin of our first parents had brought death into the world. Christ's Resurrection overcame death, "the wages of sin" (Romans 6:23). By His glorious rising from the dead, our divine Lord prefigured our own resurrection. "For if by one man's offense death reigned through one; much more they who receive abundance of grace, and of the gift, and of 146

THE RESURRECTION 

147 justice, shall reign in life through one, Jesus Christ." (Romans 5:17)

PROPHECIES FULFILLED 

Our divine Lord made many prophecies, every one of which was strikingly fulfilled. He foretold, in detail, the sufferings of His Passion and both the fact and the time of His Resurrection. "Behold, we go up to Jerusalem, and the Son of man shall be betrayed to the chief priests and the scribes: and they shall condemn Him to death. And shall deliver Him to the Gentiles to be mocked and scourged and crucified; and the third day He shall rise again." (St. Matthew 20:18-19) 

The Resurrection of our Saviour is recorded by all four Evangelists and is cited by St. Paul. There is irrefutable proof of the divinity of Christ's mission in the incident that took place when His enemies, the scribes and Pharisees, challenged Him for a sign or a miracle that would prove His claims.

Our divine Lord had insisted on His Resurrection as another proof of His divine character, and so His enemies took every precaution to place a guard around the tomb. Jesus Christ, risen from the dead, was seen repeatedly by the Apostles, by St. Mary Magdalene, and later by five hundred disciples.

KEYSTONE OF CHRISTIAN EDIFICE 

The reality of Easter - the hinge of our faith centers around the triumph of Jesus Christ, the Son of 

God, over sin and death. From the tragedy of Good

Friday to the joy of Easter Sunday the way is short. Out of the darkness and silent gloom of early morn, dawn rises and bursts into the splendor of the Resurrection. 

Our souls cry out, "He is risen! Alleluia!" (cf. St. Matthew 28:6). "I know that my Redeemer liveth, ... and in my flesh I shall see my God" (Job 19:25, 26).

A marvelous victory of faith and, indeed, a conquest of love reach out to the bewildered souls whose dim outlook now forms part of the great denial. We who have the faith through the goodness and kindness of God, and not by any merit of our own, wish to share its light with those who live in darkness. The Resurrection of

Jesus confirms His divinity. The Apostles cited the 

Resurrection of Christ as the basis of faith. 

St. Paul declares: "If Christ be not risen again, then is our preaching vain; and your faith is also vain. 

Yea, and we are found false witnesses of 

God.... If in this life only we have hope in 

Christ, we are of all men most miserable. 

But now Christ is risen from the dead." (1 Corinthians 15:14-15, 19-20) 

The compelling arguments clearly set forth in St. 

Peter's first sermon on the Resurrection bore fruitful results. Three thousand converts to Christianity were bap-

THE RESURRECTION 

149 tized that same day. To the mind of a rational man, open to reason and to the testimony of the senses, there can be no doubt about the Resurrection of Jesus Christ.

Had He appeared only once or twice, there might have been some question of illusion, or a phantom of the brain; but for forty days He appeared, not once but many times. St. Peter met Him; the ten Apostles on one occasion, without Thomas, received Him for a visit. Then, all the Apostles, including St. Thomas, saw Him. Afterwards He was seen by more than five hundred people at one time. 

"GLORY, LIFE, LIGHT" 

St. Paul, writing to the Corinthians, refers to the fact that there are many persons still living among them who had seen the risen Christ (1 Corinthians 15:6). While some of those persons might have been the victims of a delusion, it is not possible that the five hundred persons who saw Christ on one occasion should have been deceived. The Resurrection, then, is not only confirmed by the Bible; it is also an historical fact, substantiated by irrefutable proof. 

The Resurrection of our divine Saviour is one of the most consoling truths of Christianity. It re-emphasizes the promise of Jesus to the effect that we shall rise from the dead: some, of course, to eternal reproach; but the majority, we pray, to eternal happiness. Moreover, the chief motivation for our firm faith rests on this fundamental. Christ emerging victoriously from the tomb reaffirmed all that He had taught; established the divine

character of His priesthood and of His mission; made certain of the efficacy of His Sacrifice on the Cross — the reward of eternal happiness held out to those who continue faithful unto death.

The world at present is torn asunder by the spectacle of uncontrolled passion and conflicting interests. This results from irreligion, ignoring God and His laws. We too have sinned. We too must humbly beg for mercy. 

This is no time for us to imitate weak Christians, who hurt the cause of Christ and His Church. Christians unite to make sure of the only victory worth while that right shall prevail over might, to the end that peace, built on justice and charity, may be mankind's portion forevermore. Christ must reign first in your own lives, in your homes, and in the nation. Is this a vain hope?

It is the cause for which Christ died. He did not die in vain. His Resurrection is the divine assurance of the final triumph of those who follow Him.

And Jesus Christ risen from the dead is the same "yesterday and today, and... forever." Let no one make the mistake of posing as an intellectual giant in the presence of God. Rather, let us be like the little children, and pray to Him in the words of the Postcommunion prayer of the Easter Mass: "Pour upon us, O Lord, the spirit of Thy love, to make us of one mind whom, by Thy tender mercy, Thou hast filled with the paschal sacrament. Through our Lord Jesus Christ. Amen."

THE RESURRECTION 

151 Prescinding from the clear-cut evidence of the Bible, no fact in history is better established than the Resurrection of Christ. Those who refuse to accept the historical proofs would not accept the authenticity of any historian. "And, if the Spirit of Him that raised up Jesus from the dead, dwell in you; He that raised up Jesus Christ from the dead shall quicken also your mortal bodies, because of His Spirit that dwelleth in you.” (Romans 8:11)

COPIOUS REDEMPTION 

"When the goodness and kindness of God our 

Saviour appeared; not by the works of justice which we have done, but according to His mercy, He saved us by the laver of regeneration and renovation of the Holy Ghost." (Titus 3:4-5) 

From eternity, God decreed the Redemption of mankind by Jesus Christ. He intended all rational creatures to participate in its fruits. You will understand that immediately after our Risen Lord emerged from the tomb in His glorified state, His first thought was His Blessed Mother. In person, He visited her and consoled her. 

She was comforted and indeed overjoyed by the loving presence of her divine Son.

The anniversary of Easter has its own special significance. During the last few days that precede it, Christians throughout the world live over again and, in one sense, share the sorrows of our divine Redeemer. On

Easter we try to live Christ's joys with Him. In contemplating the risen Saviour, we allow our hearts to expand in adoration and in love. To our poor suffering sinful humanity, the risen Christ brings eternal hope. "If you be risen with Christ, seek the things that are above, where Christ is sitting at the right hand of God. Mind the things that are above, not the things that are upon the earth. 

For you are dead; and your life is hid with 

Christ in God." (Colossians 3:1-3) 

Christ glorified and triumphant in every age lifts aloft the banner of the Resurrection. All world plans that do not start with Christ crackle and go up in smoke.

They are like the tapestry which one affixes to the surface of a broken wall. You need the strength of stone and cement to mend the breaks. "For we are buried together with Him by baptism into death; that, as Christ is risen from the dead by the glory of the Father, so we also may walk in newness of life." (Romans 6:4)

TO RISE IN GLORY 

Jesus planned that His faithful followers would rise with the same qualities that His glorified body possessed- namely, agility, the power to go from one place to another with the swiftness of thought; subtility, the power to penetrate matteras when, after the Resurrection, our Lord entered the room where His Apostles

THE RESURRECTION 

153 had gathered ("the doors were shut"); impassibility, that is, freedom from suffering; and brightness, called "claritas." 

St. Paul here comes to our aid: "One is the glory of the sun, another the glory of the moon, and another the glory of the stars. For star differeth from star in glory.

So also is the resurrection of the dead. It is sown in corruption: it shall rise in incorruption. It is sown in dishonor: it shall rise in glory. It is sown in weakness: it shall rise in power." (1 Corinthians 15:41-43) 

Often the question is asked: When our bodies are reunited with our souls before our resurrection from the dead, what will be the condition of our bodies? The body will be beautiful, but the degree of its beauty depends upon the beauty of the soul. "For this corruptible [body] must put on incorruption; and this mortal [body] must put on immortality. And, when this mortal hath put on immortality, then shall come to pass the saying that is written: 'Death is swallowed up in victory. O death, where is thy victory? O death, where is thy sting?'' (1 Corinthians 15:53-55) 

IMMORTALITY 

Here it seems appropriate to examine the reasons for the immortality of the soul, which will be eternally pre-

served by God. Any being is, by nature, immortal which does not possess within itself any cause of dissolution. 

When we speak of natural endowments, we mean endowments that belong to the integrity of nature. Immortality is a natural endowment of the soul of man, just as man is endowed with free will and understanding. (a) The soul cannot be deprived of existence directly because the soul is interiorly spiritual that is, does not consist of component parts. Death or destruction is the dissolution of component parts. Inasmuch as the soul has no component parts, it cannot be annihilated. (b) The soul cannot be deprived of existence indirectly—that that is, by separation from the body — because as a spiritual substance independent of the body, it continues after death to perform its essential functions. 

If the soul were deprived of existence, God would contradict Himself because He has created the soul for immortality.

Since all rational men realize that there is no strict justice in this life, reason demands another life in which the good will be rewarded and the wicked who persist in defying God and do not repent, will be punished.

Independently of revelation, rational man has always held the conviction of the immortality of the soul.

Chapter XVI THE ASSUMPTION OF THE BLESSED VIRGIN MARY* PROGRAM DIRECTOR: Your Excellency, we welcome your participation in this television program. Many non- 

Catholics have expressed interest in the newly proclaimed dogma of the Assumption of the Blessed Virgin. It appears timely to invite your kindly interest in the clarification of a few questions, to the end that this new dogma of the Assumption may be better understood by our television audience. Will you, then, tell us what is meant by the formal definition of the dogma of the Assumption of the Virgin Mary? Almost every day this subject comes up for discussion. 

I assume that this recently defined dogma rests on papal infallibility. Perhaps it would be practical, first, to refresh our minds concerning the doctrine of infallibility. Can you cite authority for it in the Bible? 

ANSWER: Yes, Mr. Keen. The doctrine of papal infallibility is founded on revealed truth, notably in St. 

Matthew's Gospel. When Jesus Christ commissioned 

His Apostles and their successors to teach in His name, He said: "All power is given to Me in heaven and in 

*A Television Program over Station KFMB-TV Participants: MOST REVEREND CHARLES F. BUDDY, 

Bishop of San Diego

MR. HAROLD KEEN, Program Director 

December 11, 1950 155

earth. Going therefore, teach ye all nations: baptizing them in the name of the Father and of the Son and of the Holy Ghost. Teaching them to observe all things whatsoever I have commanded you. And behold I am with you all days, even to the consummation of the world" (St. Matthew 28:18-20). Firmly we hold that our Lord Jesus Christ was true God; otherwise the religion He established could have no binding force. Christ, the Light of the World, continues to teach through the ministry of His Church. 

Of this teaching He affirmed: "He that heareth you, heareth Me" (St. Luke 10:16). Thus it is clear that a church divinely established to teach mankind in the name of Christ would have to be safeguarded by a guarantee against error, so as to preclude the possibility of leading men astray. Moreover, Christ, who is 

Truth, cannot compromise, or appease error. Neither can His Vicar, the Pope, who of necessity must speak authoritatively with the infallible voice of truth. 

Wherefore the holy Bible further records the words of Christ that the "gates of hell shall not prevail" against His Church! (St. Matthew 16:18). But if the

Church could teach error, the gates of hell- that is, the powers of darkness - would prevail.

PROGRAM DIRECTOR: The passage you quoted is taken from the Catholic version of the Bible, is it not? 

ANSWER: Yes, Mr. Keen, but it may also be found in the 

Protestant King James translation. A comparison of the two texts shows the meaning to be substantially the same.

THE ASSUMPTION 

157 

PROGRAM DIRECTOR: Please explain what range of sub jects can be covered by the infallibility of the Pope. 

ANSWER: The range of subjects covers doctrines of faith and morals, in a word, what our divine Lord taught. 

Papal infallibility means simply that the Pope, when proclaiming a doctrine of faith or morals, either alone or united with a general council of the Church, is so guided by special divine assistance that he is preserved from error. In fact, during these nineteen hundred years no false doctrine has ever been proclaimed. To be sure, before solemnly proclaiming a doctrine of faith or morals, the Pope studies all angles of it and consults men of outstanding learning. He also gets the advice, opinion and belief of the bishops and priests and that of the 434,000,000* Catholics throughout the world.

PROGRAM DIRECTOR: Before the definition of a dogma, are Catholics free to disbelieve it? 

ANSWER: Inasmuch as before its definition the doctrine of the Assumption of the Blessed Virgin Mary was taught by the Church, Catholics would have to accept it. In a word, Catholics believe what the Church. teaches. But a formal definition takes it out of the realm of controversy and makes it a dogma or an established truth, an article of faith to be believed by all Catholics. * In 1963 the figure stands at over 558 million.

PROGRAM DIRECTOR: With this exposition I am sure we shall better understand the point at issue. What precisely, then, is meant by the dogma of the Assumption? 

ANSWER: To be accurate, permit me to read from the related Proclamation by His Holiness Pius XII, given at St. Peter's, Rome, November 1, 1950: "After we have poured forth prayers of supplication again and again, and have called upon the Spirit of Truth for the glory of Almighty God, who has lavished His special affection upon the Virgin Mary, for the honor of her Son, the Immortal King of the Ages and the Victor over sin and death, for the increase of the glory of that same august Mother and for the joy and exaltation of the entire Church: by the authority of our Lord Jesus Christ, of the blessed apostles Peter and Paul, and by our own authority, we pronounce, declare and define it to be a divinely revealed dogma that the 

Immaculate Mother of God, the ever Virgin

Mary, having completed the course of her earthly life, was assumed, body and soul, into heavenly glory."

PROGRAM DIRECTOR: May we conclude, then, Your Ex cellency, that this dogma of the Assumption of the Virgin Mary is equivalent to the discovery of a new truth? 

ANSWER: No, Mr. Keen, you could not say that. The

Assumption of the Blessed Virgin Mary was always

THE ASSUMPTION 

159 true. The great majority of the faithful throughout the world believed it for many centuries. Comparatively few held views against it. 

PROGRAM DIRECTOR: Bishop, would it be out of order for me to inquire why this dogma of the Assumption was not defined centuries ago, especially in view of its general acceptance? 

ANSWER: For reasons of prudence and also to permit further study and research, the solemn definition of a dogma may be postponed. Beyond the truth of the dogma, the question arises: Is it timely or opportune to define it? Every possible angle must be carefully weighed.

PROGRAM DIRECTOR: My recollection is that the Bible refers to Jesus' Ascension into heaven. Now why do you substitute the word "assumption" instead of "ascension" to describe the Blessed Virgin Mary's going to heaven? 

ANSWER: "Ascension" implies rising by one's own power-Jesus ascended to heaven by His own powerwhile "assumption" means to be taken up by the power of another. "Assumption" indicates that at the completion of her life here on earth the Blessed Virgin Mary "was assumed, body and soul, into heavenly glory." 

PROGRAM DIRECTOR: Do you hold that, excepting the 

Virgin Birth, Mary was a normal person?

ANSWER: Permit me to state that the Virgin Birth refers to the birth of Jesus Christ from His Virgin Mother. 

He was miraculously conceived without the intervention of a human father. The Blessed Virgin Mary was a normal person. She had a natural father and mother. 

But she was conceived and born without the stain of original sin. This special privilege of being sanctified at the instant of her conception is called her Immacu late Conception. Thus has Protestant poet Words worth immortalized this fact: "Woman! Above all women glorified - 

Our tainted nature's solitary boast."

Wordsworth, The Virgin PROGRAM DIRECTOR: Why did the Creator grant this singular dignity to Mary? 

ANSWER: Mary's Immaculate Conception, in the eternal wisdom of God, prepared her for the motherhood of 

Jesus Christ.

PROGRAM DIRECTOR: Was the assumption, or the taking up of the Virgin Mary into heaven, seen by anyone? 

ANSWER: There is no record of anyone on earth witnessing it, as far as we know. 

PROGRAM DIRECTOR: The Virgin Mary, then, was en tirely a human being? 

ANSWER: Yes, sir. 

PROGRAM DIRECTOR: And her child, Jesus, miraculously conceived was He a normal human being? If so,

THE ASSUMPTION 

161 how can you speak of Him as the Son of God? and as divine?

ANSWER: In Jesus Christ there are two natures - the divine and the human. We speak of a being according to its nature. Christ was the Son of God because of His eternal generation from God the Father. He was the Son of the Virgin Mary because He took from her His human nature. Jesus did not bring His body from heaven, but took it from Mary, His Mother. 

PROGRAM DIRECTOR: May I ask, Your Excellency, whether the Bible confirms your doctrines concerning 

Jesus and Mary?

ANSWER: Yes. Centuries before, the royal Psalmist 

(Psalm 15:10) chanted in prophecy: "Nor wilt Thou give Thy holy one to see corruption." The muchrevered Ark of the Covenant prefigured the Blessed Virgin Mary. It contained the word of God written on two tables; a portion of the bread which fell from heaven to feed the captive people in the desert; and the brazen serpent which saved these same people from death. The Ark itself was made of incorruptible wood. 

It was symbolic of the incorruptibility of Mary. 

PROGRAM DIRECTOR: That is from the Old Testament, or ancient Bible, is it not? 

ANSWER: Yes, sir.

PROGRAM DIRECTOR: Could Your Excellency quote also from the New Testament?

"FOR THEM Also” 

ANSWER: Yes, Mr. Keen. St. Luke's Gospel, Chapter I, verses 31 and 32, records the words of the Angel 

Gabriel to the Virgin Mary at the Annunciation: "Behold, thou shalt conceive... and shalt bring forth a son; and thou shalt call His name Jesus. He shall be great, and shall be called the Son of the Most High."

When the Blessed Virgin Mary gave her consent to this divine plan — in fact, at the very moment of her consent Jesus, the Second Person of the Blessed Trinity, was conceived in Mary's immaculate womb - without the intervention of man- and for nine months assumed from His Mother a human body that was never for one instant separated from Divinity. That is why

Mary is referred to as truly the Mother of God.

163 

Verse 35. The Holy Ghost shall come upon thee, and the power of the Highest shall overshadow thee: therefore also that holy thing which shall be born of thee shall be called the 

Son of God."

PROGRAM DIRECTOR: Did this miracle cover the Blessed 

Trinity?

ANSWER: Incarnation is limited to the Second Person of the Blessed Trinity. The Word, or Son of God, was never separated from the divinity hypostatically or substantially. Jesus became incarnate- not the Father or the Holy Spirit. While the Blessed Trinity was the efficient cause of the Incarnation, the Incarnation still is especially appropriated or attributed to the Holy Spirit, because it is a work of love and because it is directly related to our redemption and sanctification. If Jesus in the instant of His conception had possessed human nature only, His Mother would have conceived not God but a mere human being. 

Seven centuries before the birth of Jesus, the prophet 

Isaias had foretold His birth: "Behold, a virgin shall conceive and bear a Son" (Isaias 7:14). And later at the time of the Annunciation Mary consented, after she had the Angel's assurance that her virginity (very precious in the sight of God) would remain. 

The most ancient records refer to her as Mary, ever a virgin, for nothing is impossible to God. She is the bridge by which God comes to man; she is also the bridge by which man returns to God.

PROGRAM DIRECTOR: Bishop, is the actual Assumption of the Blessed Virgin Mary described in the Bible? 

ANSWER: No, it is not described.

PROGRAM DIRECTOR: Then is it merely a presumption? 

ANSWER: No, it is not a presumption. In fact, a divine revelation was required in order that it might be defined as a certainty. Moreover, without a divine revelation, either implicit or explicit, there could have been no definition of the Assumption as a dogma of faith—since the motive of faith must always be the authority of God in revelation.

PROGRAM DIRECTOR: Your Excellency, please tell me this. Would a private revelation suffice to establish a dogma of the Catholic Church? 

ANSWER: No. A private revelation is not acceptable for a definition of dogma.

PROGRAM DIRECTOR: Kindly indicate what is required for a public revelation. 

ANSWER: A public revelation requires apostolic origin that is to say, it must have been made known to the apostles or to one of them. The deposit of public revelation ended with the death of the last apostle.

Let us keep in mind that there are two sources of public revelation: one is the Bible; the other, Tradition. 

The truth can be contained explicitly or implicitly, either in the Bible or in Tradition. It could be contained implicitly in the Bible and explicitly in Tradition. This is the case in regard to the doctrine of the

THE ASSUMPTION 

165 Assumption of the Blessed Virgin Mary. It is found explicitly in Tradition, implicitly in the Bible; and this suffices for revelation. From these two sources, the Church through the centuries developed the reasons or the proofs that justified the definition of the Assumption.

PROGRAM DIRECTOR: Bishop, you speak of the course of the centuries. Has the doctrine of the Assumption of the Virgin Mary been recognized by the Church through the centuries? 

ANSWER: Yes, it has.

PROGRAM DIRECTOR: From what time? 

ANSWER: From the seventh century. Practically the entire Church, East and West, celebrated the Feast of the Assumption. 

PROGRAM DIRECTOR: Well, then, you would say that up to November 1, 1950, belief in the Assumption of the Blessed Virgin Mary was simply tolerated in your Church? 

ANSWER: No, you could not say that, Mr. Keen. You could not speak of a doctrine being tolerated when it was publicly taught by the liturgy of the Church and also by the Church's teaching authority. As a matter of fact, not only the priests and bishops, but also Catholics throughout the world were consulted regarding their belief. Almost unanimously the Catholics of the world reported to the Holy See their definite belief in the Assumption of the Blessed Virgin

Mary and requested its solemn definition. This universal accord of the entire Church in commemorating the Feast of the Assumption shows that what might be called her ordinary magisterium is recognized. 

PROGRAM DIRECTOR: Now, Your Excellency, may I ask if what you call the ordinary magisterium of the

Church presupposes a revelation of the doctrine? 

ANSWER: Yes. It presupposes that it has been revealed; otherwise there could be no certainty that the Blessed Mother actually entered heaven. I have no hesitation in stating further that it is probable the revelation was made to the apostles, or to one of them explicitly, because otherwise how could we explain the universal tradition in the East and in the West from the sixth century, which manifested firm belief in the Blessed Virgin Mary's Assumption? History shows that this feast was observed in Palestine before the year 500

A. D. and in Gaul during the sixth century. 

PROGRAM DIRECTOR: Your Excellency, you have stressed the observance of the Feast of the Assumption. I presume there are other proofs. 

ANSWER: Yes, there are.

PROGRAM DIRECTOR: Bishop, would you care to eluci date them?

ANSWER: Gladly. The Blessed Virgin Mary · and this is explicitly stated in the Bible and in the Book of Genesis, Chapter III, verse 15-received the fullness

THE ASSUMPTION 

167 of grace and was blessed by God in a way that excluded the least shadow of sin and therefore excluded all penalty of sin. This exceptional blessing of necessity negates the divine malediction to bring forth children in pain, and to return to dust. It follows, then, that the Blessed Virgin's body was preserved from corruption. Therefore, it did not return to dust, but must have been resuscitated in what might be called an anticipated resurrection. Moreover, many able theologians hold that concretely considered (concretely as distinguished from abstractly) the Blessed

Virgin's corporal Assumption is implicitly contained in the revealed doctrine of her divine maternity. 

PROGRAM DIRECTOR: Well, Bishop, is this argument mentioned in the Bible? 

ANSWER: Not the argument itself, but the premises of the argument are clearly revealed. Hence the logical conclusion is capable of being defined as a dogma of the Church. 

PROGRAM DIRECTOR: Do I understand, then, that the

Assumption of the Virgin Mary is implicitly revealed because of the exceptional blessings and the magnificent bestowal of God's gifts on her? 

ANSWER: Yes, if you will permit me to preface this argument by the fact that many of the Fathers of the Vatican Council of 1870 asked the then-reigning Pontiff, Pope Pius IX, to define the dogma of the Assumption. These learned prelates advanced the following argument, which, as you will see, is a compelling one.

It is generally conceded that the victory of Jesus over Satan included a triumph over sin and death; now the 

Blessed Virgin Mary - the Mother of Jesus Christ, the Son of God - was associated with her Divine Son on Calvary in His victory over sin and the author of sin. Hence theologians conclude that she is associated with Him in His victory over death by her anticipated resurrection and by her Assumption.

PROGRAM DIRECTOR: Now, Your Excellency, would you say that this second argument can be found in the Bible?

ANSWER: The premises of the conclusion are in the 

Bible, and you may consult the Epistles of St. Paul and St. John's Gospel. For example, Jesus said of Himself: "I have overcome the world" (St. John 16:33).

Again, before His sufferings and death on the Cross, Christ stated: "Now is the judgment of the world; now shall the Prince of this world be cast out. And I, if I be lifted up from the earth, will draw all things to Myself" (ibid. 12:31-32).

PROGRAM DIRECTOR: Your Excellency, precisely what was the victory over Satan and sin? 

ANSWER: The Sacrifice of the Cross as a supreme offering of love, the acceptance of humiliation and even death. He who had conquered Satan and sin on the 

Cross would conquer death by His glorious resurrection. Let me add a word that will probably be better understood - especially by the mothers of this world than would the arguments from theology and

THE ASSUMPTION 

169 Scripture. There is a very intimate relation between the mother and her son, especially as shown in the loving sympathy of a mother for a son in agonizing torments, unjustly inflicted. Had the Blessed Virgin lacked this compassion, she would have been unworthy of the motherhood of Jesus. She offered Him on the 

Cross. That is why she is sometimes referred to as Coredemptrix. The Blessed Virgin Mary, then, was intimately associated with Jesus in His sufferings and in His perfect victory over Lucifer. Therefore, she was associated with Him in the various aspects of His triumph -in His victory over sin and over death.

That victory included exemption from the corruption of the body and therefore included an anticipated resurrection and assumption into heaven. You understand that when Almighty God endows His creatures with the power of reasoning He has a right to expect them to use it. These are logical conclusions from premises that are clearly stated in the Bible. 

PROGRAM DIRECTOR: Bishop, I think you have clarified the recently defined dogma of the Assumption of the Virgin Mary, and we thank you for the time which you have graciously given to appear on this program. 

ANSWER: Before concluding, may I recite the prayer composed by our Holy Father Pope Pius XII expressly for the occasion of the proclamation of the dogma of the Assumption of the Blessed Virgin Mary? 

PROGRAM DIRECTOR: Yes, we shall be glad to hear the prayer.

ANSWER: "O Immaculate Virgin, Mother of God and Mother of men, we believe with all the fervor of our faith in your triumphal Assumption, both in body and soul, into heaven, where you are acclaimed as Queen by all the choirs of Angels and all the legions of the saints. 

And we unite with them to praise and bless the

"We trust that your merciful eyes may deign to glance down upon our miseries and our sorrows, upon our struggles and our weaknesses; that your countenance may smile upon our victories; that you may hear the voice of Jesus saying to you, of each one of us, as He once said to you of His beloved disciple: 'Behold thy Son.'

"And from the shadows of this vale of tears we seek in your heavenly assistance and tender mercy, comfort for our aching hearts and help in the trials of the Church and of our fatherland. "We believe, finally, that in the glory where you reign, clothed with the sun and crowned with the stars, you are, after Jesus, the joy and gladness of all the angels and of all the saints. "And from this earth, over which we tread as pilgrims, comforted by our faith in future resurrection, we look to you, our life, our sweetness and our hope; draw us onward with the sweetness of your voice, that one day, after our exile, you may show us Jesus, the blessed Fruit of your womb, O clement, O loving, O sweet 

Virgin Mary!"

Chapter XVII THE CHURCH THE 

HE CHURCH (from the Greek "kyriakon," the 

Lord's house) is the mankind-embracing society founded by Jesus Christ to teach the truths He revealed, to administer the sacraments He instituted, and thereby to lead men to heaven. It includes all those who are baptized, who partake of the same sacraments and who acknowledge the primacy of the Pope, the Bishop of Rome. 

Objectively, the Catholic Church is divinely established to channel the grace of God for the salvation of souls. Subjectively, she, the Bride of Christ, may be considered as the communion of saints- i. e., the Church militant here on earth; the Church suffering in purgatory; and the Church triumphant in heaven. Another distinction clarifies the subject: Externally man enters the 

Church through faith and baptism. He accepts the truths revealed by God through Jesus Christ. Internally or spiritually, to be a Catholic means union with God through grace: "The kingdom of God is within you." (St. Luke 17:21)

Through the course of centuries, heresies and schisms have hurt the cause of religion. Notable among these are the Greek schism of the eleventh century, which split the East and West of Europe; the Protestant heresy of 172

THE CHURCH 

173 the sixteenth century, which brought about a rift in the 

West of Europe. Heresy (from the Greek "hairesis," to prefer) is the denial of any truth revealed by Christ.

Schism (from the Greek "schisma," to split) marks a division or separation from the Mother Church on the part of those who refuse to acknowledge the lawful authority of the Pope and bishops.

WHEAT AND COCKLE 

When He compared the Church to a field in which cockle grew alongside the wheat, our Saviour was predicting the schisms and heresies that were destined to afflict His Church. "Another parable He proposed to them, saying: 'The kingdom of heaven is likened to a man that sowed good seed in his field.' 

(St. Matthew 13:24)

Jesus Christ, Son of God, firmly established His Church on earth to instruct, to sanctify and guide man to heaven. 

He commissioned the Apostles to teach: "And He said to them: 'Go ye into the whole world and preach the Gospel to every creature.' (St. Mark 16:15)

In the continuation of the sacred priesthood and the holy Mass, together with the seven sacraments, Christ richly endowed His Church with the means of sanctification.

"... teaching them to observe all things whatsoever I have commanded you. And behold

I am with you all days, even to the consummation of the world." (St. Matthew 28:20) "He said therefore to them again: 'Peace be to you. As the Father hath sent Me, I also send you.'" (St. John 20:21) "When He had said this, He breathed on them: ... 'Receive ye the Holy Ghost.' 

(St. John 20:22) "And taking bread, He gave thanks and brake and gave to them, saying: "This is My body, which is given for you. Do this for a commemoration of Me.'" (St. Luke 22:19) "You shall be holy... because I the Lord am holy." 

(Leviticus 20:26) DIVINE AND HUMAN 

The Church is both human and divine. Human because it is composed of men, women and children with all weaknesses, infidelities and failures of human nature - whence result schisms, heresies and scandals. 

The Church is divine in her Founder, her origin. The

THE CHURCH 

175 Church is supernatural in her objective to lead people to heaven, supernatural in her means of sanctification, prayer and the graces always made available in the sacraments - all of which fulfills her mission. 

The Vatican Council (1870) clarifies the above preamble: "In order to perpetuate the saving work of redemption, the eternal Pastor and Bishop of souls decreed to establish a holy Church, in which all the faithful might be gathered together by the unity of faith and love, as in the house of the living God." (1870, Session 4)

FAITHFUL IN TEACHING 

The Old Testament tells how God provided for the enlightenment and spiritual direction of the people. He also guarded the pre-Christian revelations by appointing the patriarchs to guard their integrity. Later a group of teachers from the synagogue were entrusted with them. 

Even though these latter fell heir to vices, they were always faithful in teaching the true doctrines announced by Moses. On this point, recall the words of Jesus: "The scribes and the Pharisees have sitten on the chair of Moses. All things therefore whatsoever they shall say to you, observe and do." (St. Matthew 23:2-3)

FIVE HUNDRED WITNESSES 

The first authentic history of the Church may be found in the Acts of the Apostles, which starts by recording the Ascension of Christ into heaven. During the missionary career of our divine Saviour here on earth, more than five hundred disciples, most of them unlettered and lacking influence, accepted His doctrines. The leaders of these disciples were rough, humble, honest fishermen, timid until they received the Holy Spirit on 

Pentecost Sunday. Fortified by the sacrament of Confirmation, they became enlightened, courageous, heroic. 

In simple words our divine Lord told the Apostles the story of the Redemption. He directed them to tell it to the world, just as He had told it to them. Note that 

Jesus did not depend on written documents. In fact, He left no written words. Christ established His Church to teach mankind the truths of salvation and make divine help available to everyone. He appointed St. Peter to direct this Church. Before He selected His Apostles, Jesus spent a night in prayer. "And it came to pass, in those days, that He went out into a mountain to pray, and He passed the whole night in the prayer of God. 

And when day was come, He called unto 

Him His disciples; and He chose twelve of them (whom He also named Apostles)." (St. Luke 6:12-13)

THE CHURCH 

177 

"THOU ART PETER" 

When appointing St. Peter head of the Church, Jesus said: "And I say to thee that: "Thou art Peter, and upon this rock I will build My Church. And the gates of hell shall not prevail against it. 

And I will give to thee the keys of the kingdom of heaven. And whatsoever thou shalt bind upon earth, it shall be bound also in heaven; and whatsoever thou shalt loose on earth, it shall be loosed also in heaven.' (St. Matthew 16:18-19) 

Note our Saviour's promise to the Twelve: "Amen, I say to you that you, who have followed Me, in the regeneration when the Son of man shall sit on the seat of His majesty, you also shall sit on twelve seats judging the twelve tribes of Israel." (St. Matthew 19:28)

The Apostles received the sublime privilege of ordination to the sacred priesthood when Jesus instituted the 

Holy Eucharist. 

THE SPIRIT OF TRUTH 

On this occasion Christ promised to send the Holy Spirit to teach them all truth and to abide with them forever.

"But when He, the Spirit of truth, is come,

He will teach you all truth. For He shall not speak of Himself; but what things soever He shall hear He shall speak. And the things that are to come He shall show you.... All things whatsoever the Father hath are Mine.... Therefore I said that He shall receive of Mine and show it to you. 

A little while, and now you shall not see Me: and again a little while, and you shall see 

Me: because I go to the Father." (St. John 16:13, 15-16) "GOING, ...

TEACH... 

“And the eleven disciples went into Galilee, unto the mountain where Jesus had appointed them..., and Jesus coming, spoke to them, saying: 'All power is given to Me in heaven and in earth. Going, therefore, teach ye all nations: baptizing them in the name of the

Father and of the Son, and of the Holy Ghost.'" (St. Matthew 28:16, 18-19)

The promise of our Lord was directed to the Apostles and to the indefectible teaching authority of the Church, which was to continue to the end of time. In fact, the Apostles, exercising their authority, ordained priests, consecrated bishops, and sent them into various regions to preach what they had learned from Jesus. St.

THE CHURCH 

179 Paul and St. Barnabas ordained priests for the missions of the Church. "And when they had ordained to them priests in every church and had prayed with fasting, they commended them to the Lord, in whom they believed." 

(Acts of the Apostles 14:22) 

St. Paul instructed, ordained and consecrated his coworker, St. Timothy: "A faithful saying: 'If a man desire the office of a bishop, he desireth a good work.' It behoveth therefore a bishop to be blameless, the husband of one wife, sober, prudent, of good behavior, chaste, given to hospitality, a teacher." (1 Timothy 3:1-2) 

In a letter to St. Timothy, St. Paul follows up: "And the things which thou hast heard of me by many witnesses, the same commend to faithful men who shall be fit to teach others also." (2 Timothy 2:2) 

A record also exists of St. Paul's instruction to St. Titus: "For this cause I left thee in Crete: that thou shouldst set in order the things that are wanting and shouldst ordain priests in every city, as I also appointed thee. (Titus 1:5)

PENTECOST 

Our Saviour's promise to send the Holy Spirit was fulfilled ten days after His Ascension into heaven. 

Pentecost is a Greek word meaning "fiftieth" — it was a solemn feast of the Jews, so called because celebrated fifty days after the end of the Passover. The Jewish people made it a day of thanksgiving for the wheat harvest and for the table of laws given to Moses on Mount 

Sinai. The Christian Pentecost, fifty days after Easter, commemorates the descent of the Holy Ghost and the establishment of the Church.

The Apostles had made a retreat. Accompanied by the sound of a violent wind coming from above, the Holy Spirit appeared in the form of tongues of fire over the head of each one, including the Blessed Virgin Mary, the Apostles, the holy women and the one hundred twenty disciples who had made the retreat with them. "And when they were come in they went up into an upper room, where abode Peter and John, James and Andrew, Philip and 

Thomas, Bartholomew and Matthew, James of Alpheus and Simon Zelotes and Jude the brother of James. All these were persevering with one mind in prayer, with the women and Mary the mother of Jesus, and with His brethren... (now the number of persons together was about a hundred and twenty).” 

(Acts of the Apostles 1:13-14, 15)

THE CHURCH 

181 

PARTED TONGUES 

It is the opinion of theologians that the parted tongues of fire have a special significance. (a) Fire illumines, gives light, diffuses brightness and banishes darkness, as do the truths of Christianity. (b) Fire purifies, removes the dross of sin. Gold is tried in fire. (c) Fire warms. The gifts of the Holy Ghost warm frozen hearts and inflame them with divine love.

God's eternal designs rest on reason and have significance. The parted tongues may also signify the variety of talents, including the languages, with which the Holy 

Spirit gifted the disciples in the cenacle. "To one indeed, by the Spirit, is given the word of wisdom: and to another, the word of knowledge, according to the same Spirit; to another, faith in the same Spirit; to another, the grace of healing in one Spirit; to another, the working of miracles; to another, prophecy; to another, the discerning of spirits; to another, diverse kinds of tongues; to another, interpretation of speeches. But all these things, one and the same Spirit worketh, dividing to every one according as He will." (1 Corinthians 12:8-11) 

SYMBOLS OF THE HOLY SPIRIT 

The fact that the parted tongues rested on the head of each one typifies the abiding of the Holy Spirit in the

Church, and the specific charge made to the Apostles and to the priests of the Catholic Church to teach the word of God.

The mighty wind symbolized the operation of the Holy Ghost and reminds us that the wind is swift, invisible, and penetrates distant places. Thus the Holy 

Spirit directed the spread of the faith over the earth. "And suddenly there came a sound from heaven, as of a mighty wind coming; and it filled the whole house where they were sitting." 

(Acts of the Apostles 2:2) 

The Catholic Church began its mission on Pentecost Sunday. The Holy Spirit did not cease His indwelling on Pentecost Sunday, for He is the love of God who continues to dwell in all Christians and in every good work.

On the first Pentecost Sunday, St. Peter, being endued with the Holy Spirit, baptized three thousand converts. 

The Third Person of the Blessed Trinity enriched the 

Church by inspiring the Bible and Apostolic Tradition. 

The Holy Ghost governs and protects the Church, directing both clergy and laity, dispensing divine grace. "But the Paraclete, the Holy Ghost, whom the 

Father will send in My name, He will teach you all things and bring all things to your mind, whatsoever I shall have said to you." (St. John 14:26)

THE CHURCH 

183 

THE FIRST POPE 

The Church was firmly established as a permanent institution with St. Peter as its head. When our divine 

Lord came to the actual formation of His Church, He designated St. Peter as the first Pope: "Thou art Simon the son of Jona. Thou shalt be called Cephas, which is interpreted Peter." (St. John 1:42) "Peter" (from the Greek "petros," a rock) indicates a permanent, visible, and substantial foundation. Faithful 

Catholics acknowledge the primacy of the Pope, accept all the truths revealed by Christ, and partake of the sacraments He instituted. In the Bible we note the use of the plural "churches," which points to several different assemblies. But the singular term "My Church" obviously designates the congregation of all the faithful. ... Christ also loved the Church and delivered Himself up for it; that He might sanctify it, cleansing it by the laver of water in the word of life.” (Ephesians 5:25-26)

DIVINELY ESTABLISHED 

To re-emphasize by repetition, you may define the Catholic Church as the divinely organized assembly of all those who accept and practice the faith taught by Jesus Christ, and who obey their lawful pastors under the Pope, the visible head of the Church. Through the

course of centuries, the Catholic Church has come to be known as "the Kingdom of God, the Vineyard, the 

Sheepfold, the Mystical Body of Christ, the Bride of

Christ." The primitive, patriarchal and Mosaic revelations of the Old Testament served as a foreshadowing and preparation for the Messias. The Catholic Church of the New Testament fulfills the prophecies foretold of it. 

God is the Author of the true religion, which is coordinated and unified in its dogmas, in its liturgy (public worship), and in its objectives. He sent Jesus, the 

Messias, as Mediator to firmly establish this Church, which holds the divine mandate to teach all nations. 

He clearly outlined the virtues to be exemplified by His followers, taught the liturgy to be observed, and firmly established the objectives of His Church. He appointed 

St. Peter and his successors to be the interpreters and the guardians of the Deposit of Faith. This fact in itself demands one true religion. Jesus Christ, representing His 

Father in heaven, briefed the twelve Apostles both before and following His Resurrection. He gave them the command and the power to teach, to govern and to administer the sacraments.

THE MYSTICAL BODY 

Our divine Redeemer intended that all men should belong to the Church. Membership in the Church, or what is more accurately known as the Mystical Body of 

Christ, requires baptism.

Jesus Christ, who promised to be with the Church until the end of time, is the invisible Head, while the

THE CHURCH 

185 Pope, the Vicar of Christ and the Bishop of Rome, is the visible head of the Church. The word "Pope" means "Father." He is the spiritual father of all the faithful and the supreme head of the Church. Bishops (from the 

Greek "episkopos," meaning "overseer") are the successors of the Apostles, and by virtue of their consecration possess the plenitude of the priesthood. Charged with the grave responsibility of cultivating the spiritual welfare of the flock committed to his care-the faithful of the diocese and with protecting the properties of the Church, the bishop must constantly seek the guidance of the Holy Spirit of Wisdom, who loves the Church, the 

Bride of Christ. 

---

Chapter XVIII  

MARK is an inherent quality by which a person or thing can be known. The God of Wisdom has provided definite and unmistakable visible signs by which His Church can be recognized. Inquirers may analyze the obvious marks of unity, sanctity, universality and apostolicity; marks which no man-made religion may justly claim.

The marks of the Church are not afterthoughts superadded, like citations won in war. They are endowments born with the Church, inseparable from her essence. In the mission Christ entrusted to His Apostles, He stressed these four notes to serve as efficient signs by which the Church can be discovered. The Bible clarifies this fundamental doctrine. 

A renowned convert observed: "These notes are, as anyone knows who has looked into the subject, certain great and simple characteristics which He who founded the Church has stamped upon her in order to draw both the reason and the imagination of men to her as being really a divine work, and a religion distinct from all other religious communities" (Newman, Apologia, Appendix VI). 

The Nicene Creed includes the four marks which distinguish the Catholic Church. "I believe... in the one, holy, catholic and apostolic Church." The Bible 186

teaches us that the Holy Spirit unites all who are baptized: "For in one Spirit were we all baptized into one body.. (1 Corinthians 12:13). We are therefore members under one invisible Head. Keep in mind, however, that the soul and body of the Church do not make two separate churches, but only one, just as the soul and body of man form one rational person. The attributes and marks of the Church differ inasmuch as an attribute is generally invisible while a mark is visible. 

THE UNITY OF THE CHURCH 

Christ taught: "And other sheep I have that are not of this fold: them also I must bring. And they shall hear My voice, and there shall be one fold and one Shepherd." (St. John 10:16)

Our divine Lord referred to His Church as "a kingdom," "a city," "a household," "an edifice" built solidly on a rock. These designations remove all doubt regarding the unity of an enduring structure destined to defy time and persecution. St. Paul writes of this organized body of which Christ is the Head: "From whom the whole body, being compacted and fitly joined together, by what every joint supplieth, according to the operation in the measure of every part, maketh increase of the body, unto the edifying of itself in charity." (Ephesians 4:16)

To ensure this end, by a unified doctrine Christ consolidated the government of the entire Church into the primacy of supreme authority, vested in the Pope. St. 

Peter and the Apostles had a meeting of minds and acted in unison to co-ordinate and teach. 

Moreover, our Saviour prayed that the members of

His Church would persevere in this unity. "And not for them only do I pray, but for them also who through their word shall believe in Me. That they all may be one, as

Thou, Father, in Me, and I in Thee; that they also may be one in us; that the world may believe that Thou hast sent Me." (St. John 17:20-21)

St. Paul emphasized this point, so clearly manifest in one faith, one governing body, one worship, and in the charity which unites all the members of the Church. cc "... Careful to keep the unity of the Spirit in the bond of peace. One body and one Spirit: as you are called in one hope of your calling. 

One Lord, one faith, one baptism.” 

(Ephesians 4:3-5) 

In the liturgy one may easily discern the unity of the Church, which is directed by one government and regulated by a uniform doctrine in force throughout the world. The sacraments, the Holy Sacrifice of the Mass, and all the other essential means of sanctification remain unchanged simply because the successors of St. Peter and

the Apostles continue to observe whatever Christ commanded. There may be different minor details that fit various climes and emergencies, but these relate to discipline-not to doctrine. 

Note a uniform charity in action throughout the world. "By this shall all men know that you are My disciples, if you have love one for another." (St. John 13:35)

Moreover, the Church reflects unity in her infallible teaching the doctrines being protected, explained and interpreted by the magisterium of the Church. While truth remains the same, the Church makes progress through the faithful who learn her sacred doctrines and extend the kingdom of God on earth. 

"Teach Ye All NATIONS” 

By way of personal experience, during my years in Rome a striking example of the universality and the unity of the Church came to light. One of our fellow students, converted from the ministry of the Anglican Church, remarked: "While studying for the ministry in the Protestant School of Divinity, fourteen seminarians in my class listened to nineteen different opinions re the same doctrine of the Church of England. Here in Rome there are over six thousand young men from all over the world preparing themselves for the sacred priesthood. 

These seminarians speak more than thirty-nine different languages. But all hear lectures in the Latin language

of the Church, the one doctrine revealed by Jesus Christ. 

Once ordained, these young missionaries, fired with zeal, travel to the ends of the world, literally fulfilling the command of Christ engraved on the keystone of the arch over the main entrance to the University: "Euntes docete omnes gentes Going therefore, teach ye all nations" (St. Matthew 28:19).

THE SANCTITY OF THE CHURCH 

The Catholic Church is holy because her divine Founder, Jesus Christ, is holy and the fountainhead of sanctity. Holiness means nearness to God. That is, a man is holy if united to God by love; a day is holy if consecrated to God. The Church is holy and obedient, subject to her divine Founder. "... that He might present it to Himself, a glorious Church, not having spot or wrinkle, or any such thing; but that it should be holy and without blemish.” (Ephesians 5:27)

The Church exemplifies holiness because she possesses the means that sanctify, principally in the Holy Mass and the sacraments. For example, the Church has a perfect right to declare herself holy because of the eminent sanctity of so many millions of her members; not only canonized saints, but those who by virtue of baptism become branches of the Vine that is Christ, and are further sanctified by the Holy Spirit, who dwells in them, and by the frequent and worthy reception of Holy Communion.

"I am the vine: you the branches." (St. John 15:5) "Be ye holy, because I the Lord your God am holy." (Leviticus 19:2) "For this is the will of God, your sanctification." 

(1 Thessalonians 4:3) THE CATHOLICITY OF THE CHURCH 

The word "catholic" means "universal." The Church is catholic because she exists, at least potentially, in all places, in all ages, and manifests all truth. ... preach the Gospel to every creature.” (St. Mark 16:15)

Christ intended that the zealous interest of His Apostles and their successors should embrace mankind.

The depressing spectacle of five hundred Protestant sects all claiming to be followers of Christ and to represent Him—yet all rejecting the authority of the Pope, the Vicar of Christ on earth, including the deposit of faith entrusted to him- obviously was never intended by our divine Redeemer.

In the second century St. Ignatius, Bishop of Antioch and martyr, wrote: "Wherever Christ is, there is the Catholic Church."

Pope Pius XI taught that "Christ must reign in the hearts of men, women, and children; not only in the 

Church, but in the homes, the school, the hospital,

the city council, the marketplace, in the offices, theaters, playgrounds, wherever men meet; in the study of the writer, in the laboratory of the scientist, in the artist's studio, in the kitchen - wherever men and women live, endure, suffer and die. There is no justification for the cleavage between faith and science, religion and culture, life and worship."

THE CHURCH IS APOSTOLIC 

Apostolicity means that the Catholic Church throughout the ages continues to live and teach the same doctrines taught by Jesus to His Apostles and their successors. This union and concordance with the Apostolic body identifies the true Church and gives her the mark of apostolicity. Note here, Christ founded His Church upon the visible foundation of the Apostles. 

It must be evident, then, that those groups which cut themselves off from the authority of the Bishop of Rome, the lawful successor of St. Peter, and tried to revise the doctrine taught by Jesus cannot pretend to be the true Church. None of the sects or schismatic churches which have fallen away from the Mother Church has the four marks. 

Jesus Christ, the divine Founder of the Catholic

Church, made apostolicity one of her notes. This means that the Apostles were the rocks of the foundation; that they had an integral part in the Church's constitution and carried out the divine mandate to "go and teach.”

The very zeal of the Apostles fired them to suffer all

hardships, even persecution, torture and death for Jesus Christ and for the souls committed to their care. 

To further broaden our knowledge of the apostolicity of the Church, let us reflect that its divine Founder appointed the Apostles the first stones of the edifice. Their lawful successors through these nineteen centuries show continuity from the Apostles and the divine message they taught. In selecting this group of humble but stalwart fishermen to be His associates, Jesus made them the foundation on which to build an enduring temple to preserve and extend the fruits of the Redemption. 

Experience proves that those nations which neglected to extend the gift of faith to heathen lands saw their own decline.

DOERS OF THE WORD 

Religion is not static. No real Catholic can pretend to follow Christ and be indifferent to the spiritual distress of others. Probably the most insulting thing a creature ever said to his Creator was from the lips of the murderer Cain: "Am I my brother's keeper?" (Genesis 4:9) 

To answer a related question often posed: Could the 

Blessed Virgin Mary be counted an apostle? She gave 

Jesus to mankind. She leads us to the same divine Redeemer. "To Jesus through Mary." 

Father Mateo Crawley-Boevey, SS. CC., of happy memory, explains this apostolicity in relation to the 

Blessed Virgin Mary. He observes that while St. Peter

presided over the Upper Room, where a hundred and twenty disciples had gathered in spiritual exercises to prepare for the coming of the Holy Spirit, the Blessed

Mother was there as Queen of Apostles. Mary, the Virgin Mother of Jesus, pondered in her Immaculate Heart the mysteries of the Incarnation of Jesus, His infancy, His thirty years at Nazareth. Father Mateo, who defines zeal as "divine heat radiating from a divine fire," says that substantially to be an apostle means: "with Mary and like Mary to give Jesus to souls; with Mary and like 

Mary to give souls to Jesus."*

SOURCE OF VITALITY 

The Catholic Church alone has the four marks which belong to the true Church. The Catholic Church is called 

Roman because the Pope, the Bishop of Rome, resides in Rome. The Church derives her enduring vitality and infallible authority from the Holy Spirit, who guides and preserves the Church as one, holy, catholic and apostolic by dwelling in her and sanctifying her members throughout the world. While Christ promised to be with the
